ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u8	It concerns the abolition of unrecoverable VAT, NPR VAT. No impact studies have been carried out on this measure. In the current climate in which companies mainly need a fast cash flow rather than a possible overseas investment fund, it would be wiser to delete the article.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u10	The idea is very simple: the concept of a recoverable VAT that has never been paid is, however, a little special!

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u12	This amendment, which I am defending on behalf of Huguette Bello, concerns the abolition of NPR VAT. This abolition follows the same logic as the reduction in the tax reduction provided for in Article 4: making the overseas countries themselves finance their own development. Our colleague points out that taxpayers will be able to participate, to a large extent, in the 20 per cent increase in the budget announced by the Government, thus replacing the necessary national solidarity in the planned investment. In this way, the most fragile territories are asked to self-finance their development, which is not acceptable, especially in the light of the economic and social climate in La Réunion.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u13	I call Mr Eric Coquerel to support Amendment No. 597.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u14	I will rely on about the same arguments. The abolition of the scheme, whose cost is estimated at between EUR 220 million and EUR 250 million per year, will handicap the overseas territories. I do not believe that the time has come to adopt such an article, especially since, as Mr Lorion has said, there has been no impact assessment. We therefore do not fully understand the purpose of this article, which is likely to ignite the overseas countries and territories again in the face of a situation which they often consider unfair.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u24	I am defending this amendment by my colleague, Lénaick Adam, who has been chosen in the riding. It aims to support the newly created Guyanese companies by allowing them to benefit for eight years, from the date of their creation, from a tax rebate on their profits in the framework of the free zones of new-generation activity. Particular attention to Guyanese entrepreneurs is crucial: 22 per cent of the working population is unemployed in that territory. In order to maintain the current unemployment rate, which is already particularly high, by 2030 60,000 jobs would have to be created, and by 2040 110,000 jobs would have to be created. The employment of young people, the first victims of unemployment, is a challenge for Guyana, and entrepreneurship is a solution to combat this mass unemployment. Let us therefore try to encourage business starters in their efforts. With this amendment, my colleague would like to see further support for the creation of businesses in his territory.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u25	The rapporteur-general has the floor to support Amendment No. 795, which is the subject of several sub-amendments, Nos. 1221, 1253, 1267, 1223, 1222, 1254 and 1224. Subamendments Nos. 1253 and 1267 are identical, as are subamendments Nos. 1222 and 1254.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u26	It aims to restore the text of the National Assembly on the development of TEOM, the tax on the removal of household waste.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u27	The sub-amendments Nos. 1221 and 1223 of Courson may be presented in a consolidated format.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u28	Sub-amendment No 1221 proposes to secure legally the issues relating to TEOM. Indeed, in recent years, many case law has weakened this tax by entailing cancellations of rates and by making it difficult to finance the public service. Article 7 is an appropriate means of securing the deliberations of local executive bodies. However, it is possible to further secure TEOM. It is therefore important to define precisely the current jurisprudential concept of "a rate which is clearly not disproportionate to the amount of expenditure" developed by the Council of State, which is the only one admitted. In concrete terms, the inclusion in Article 1520 of the General Tax Code of a clarification according to which "a 15 % disproportion is allowed between the proceeds of the tax and the expenditure" appears appropriate, since the ceiling rate of coverage of 115 % is little or no that resulting from the case-law: this would make it possible to integrate the expenses incurred indirectly under the support functions. Ladies and gentlemen, you know that case law has overturned the deliberations of local assemblies setting the rate of TEOM on the grounds that it was on balance. We propose that you be secured by saying that, as long as there is no more than 15% difference, this rate is in accordance with the law. Subamendment No 1223 has a completely different purpose. The current wording of paragraph 6 of the article appears to be insufficiently precise in the light of the jurisprudence of the Council of State. Indeed, in its most recent decision of 19 March 2018, the Conseil d'État distinguishes between the expenditure incurred by the public service for the collection and treatment of household and similar waste and the expenditure incurred by the general administration of the community or the public institution exercising that competence, which cannot be financed by the tax. However, public authorities or institutions exercising the responsibility for collection and processing often assume other powers, by pooling the supporting functions between the different public services they have to perform. This sub-amendment therefore allows the full cost of the service to be taken into account by integrating not only direct service delivery costs but also indirect management costs into actual operating expenses.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u29	Mrs Pires Beaune has the floor in support of sub-amendment No 1253.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u30	Article 7 is, as I said, welcome but not precise enough. This sub-amendment is intended to replace the words "the waste collection and treatment department" with the broader words "mission-related". This would provide greater security for community deliberations.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u31	Mr Jean-René Cazeneuve has the floor in support of sub-amendment No 1267.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u32	The aim is to take structural loads into account as a whole. Article 7 is a step forward, but this sub-amendment makes it possible to go even further. It is proposed to open up the possibility of a ratio corresponding to the share of expenditure related to waste collection and treatment in overall expenditure, in order to have a view of the total cost of TEOM.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u33	I call Mr Charles de Courson to support sub-amendment No. 1222.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u34	It also aims to secure this service. Paragraphs 9 to 11 provide for the taking over by public authorities or public institutions of inter-municipal cooperation – EPCI – of the reductions resulting from the finding, by a court decision, of the illegality of the deliberations taken by the municipality or the EPCI in respect of the garbage collection tax. This measure is not justified, on the one hand, because the deliberations on TEOM do not escape the control of legality exercised by the prefectures over the local budgets, on the other, because the State now receives 3.6% of TEOM in exchange for the costs of relief.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u35	Mrs Pires Beaune has the floor in support of sub-amendment 1254.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u36	It is intended, by deleting paragraphs 10 to 12, to remove the responsibility of the communities for the relief in the event of an unfavourable court decision, on the ground that the review of legality must be carried out, and that the State should be charged management costs in return for the discounts it provides to the communities.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u37	I call Mr Charles de Courson to support sub-amendment No. 1224.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u38	It also deals with the conditions under which TEOM's rebates are to be paid to the communities, conditions which need to be clarified. If the State has never granted an ex officio relief in respect of the disputes in question to taxpayers who have not made a formal appeal, it must be ensured that ex officio or simple reliefs cannot be pronounced without the possibility of recourse by the community when the disputes are brought before it. Communities should also be able to monitor, particularly in the context of testing the certification of accounts, the rebates imposed on them by making it possible to check the consistency between the least revenue found in the twelfths of taxation and the decisions of the tax judge. Finally, this sub-amendment limits the reductions pronounced, starting from TEOM's 2019 taxation, to the sole share of TEOM that the tax judge has estimated to be surplus, thus providing a legal basis for the partial maintenance of the tax.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u76	Some hydro-electric concessions that have expired have not yet been renewed. The Senate introduced into the text a new fee applicable as of January 1, 2019. The amendment amends the wording of the Senate for the following reasons. The new fee, which was originally to be based on the turnover of each of the extended concessions, finally relates to the revenues of the only concession beneficiaries. This change in its base will lead to a marked disparity between the territories and local authorities hosting extended hydro-electric concessions, i.e. in "slippery" time frames. Indeed, some of them will receive the amount of the fee, while others will be excluded, depending on the profitability of the facilities, which is in no way the responsibility of the communities, you will grant it to us. The amendment is therefore intended to revert to the common law of a fee proportional to the turnover of the concessions. For example, all territories where hydro-electric concessions remain on a rolling schedule will benefit from the fee. In addition, the rate would be capped at 7 % of turnover, which would in any event lead to a revenue higher than the EUR 10 million mentioned by the Court of Auditors in its analysis note on the implementation of the 2017 budget for trade account 914 on the renewal of hydro-electric concessions.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u80	This is a fallback amendment. Article 9 bis, introduced by the Senate, introduces a fee on concessions in slippery periods, the rate of which is set at 50% of the normative result. Even by applying this rate to the result after tax on companies, the provision voted by the Senate would lead to a tax equivalent to two thirds of the result, an amount which could be declared confiscatory by the Constitutional Council, especially since, in order to assess the confiscatory nature of a tax, it is necessary also to take into account the other taxes of the person liable – in this case, in particular, local taxation, which amounts to about a quarter of the revenues of hydropower installations. The amendment therefore proposes to amend the scheme in order to introduce a 50% tax on the result, including both the taxation on companies and the new levy on concessions in slippery periods.

The territories that are said to be forgotten in the Republic – once referred to as the diagonal of emptiness, and even if this term is no longer used by sociologists and geographers, it is clear what this is all about – suffer the consequences of successive declines in the State's allocations to local authorities. This year, the decrease is EUR 467 million. I will be told that it is little compared to the $3.9 billion recorded in 2017, but I was going to say that it is too much that makes the thing unbearable. That is why article 23 is so important. We cannot continue to ensure that the state outsources austerity to local and regional authorities, especially since, if I am not mistaken, the 13 billion savings plan provided for in the Public Finance Programming Act for the years 2018-2022 provides not only for a reduction in allocations, but also for austerity obligations, which will have a direct impact on the solidarity mechanisms of the local and regional authorities benefiting our citizens. For all these reasons, we are opposed to Article 23. We call for a halt to the reorganisation of local and regional authorities' revenues. It seems to me at least as important as abandoning the carbon tax, even if our citizens suffer the consequences indirectly.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u167	For more than 20 years, the State has been monitoring the allocations of local authorities by setting an overall standard of variation, which is ensured by a puncture on some of them, used as an adjustment variable. Until the mid-1990s, each State contribution was free to evolve in a manner that was specific to inflation, growth and the amount of the overall operating allocation. However, the general review of public policies – the RGPP –, the sacrosanct principle of resorption of the public deficit below the 3% mark and, more generally, the Maastricht convergence criteria have brought the state, in relation to its relationship with local authorities, into a logic of delegated austerity that strangles those where social or territorial needs are most pressing. This loss of tax revenues leads to the introduction of a smoothing mechanism over time. This amendment aims to reduce the scope of Article 25. So I would tend to consider it not just editorial.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u276	I have before me several amendments, Nos 515, 517, 518, 519 and 521, which can be presented in a consolidated format. Mr Raphaël Schellenberger has the floor to support them.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u277	Rule 25aa responds in part to this series of amendments, which makes interesting proposals. The provisions of Article 25, designed with a view to the closure of the Fessenheim nuclear power plant and certain coal-fired power stations, anticipate the disappearance of large tax revenues in dependent territories. The Fessenheim site was chosen to install a nuclear power plant there, not because it was a megalopolis, but because it was at a distance from many areas and could thus participate in the energy planning of the territory. Nuclear power plants were not randomly positioned on French territory. This territory is now entirely dependent on the nuclear power plant. The closure of such an industrial tool, which is also efficient, once decided, takes time for the territory to bounce back. This is the spirit of Article 25, which can be amended to make it more effective. My amendments thus aim to interest the State in the conversion of the territory, in particular that of Fessenheim. If the State is more firmly committed to economic conversion, by accompanying communities to create wealth through the establishment of new economic activities, it will have to compensate for revenue losses for less. On the contrary, if it allows the actors of the territory to manage, while it is known that its intervention is necessary in these competitions for the installation of economic activities, it will have to compensate longer for the loss of revenues greater. [[Applause on the benches of the UDI-Agir group]] This is the spirit of these various amendments, which aim first to propose a comprehensive wording of Article 25, regardless of the territory concerned by this type of event, before targeting the only Fessenheim territory.

In 2010, the reform of the trade tax had frozen the country's contribution to national equalization. The territory has long become a contributor to the FNGIR, since the foundations of the FNGIR are frozen at the time of the abolition of the business tax. In the meantime, however, an economic circumstance may arise, which causes the tax-producing event to disappear – for example, the State decides to abolish the Fessenheim nuclear power plant. However, the territorial community will continue to contribute to the tax revenue it is supposed to receive as a result of an economic activity that has disappeared. That's the problem!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u282	Exactly!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u283	The mechanism introduced by the Senate, while remaining optional, opens the possibility of rediscussing the amounts due to the FNGIR for a contributing territory when the tax base that generates this contribution disappears. The article, without being binding, at least opens the way for discussion between the territories concerned and the tax administration. While my amendments aim to go further in this direction, the article proposes at least a balanced measure of common sense, to which the Ministry of Ecological and Solidarity Transition would rather be in favour.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u403	The Finance Committee's amendment plans to reverse the progress made by chambers of commerce and industry in the Senate by reinstating the reduction of the room fee by EUR 100 million for 2019 and EUR 200 million from 2020. The ITC tax is now down 46% compared to 2012. This percentage will rise to 53% if the new decrease for 2019 is voted. These successive declines have a disastrous impact on community support, on the dynamism of the territories and on the redistributive effect of the tax, which now allows smaller businesses to benefit from services financed by large companies. But above all, it is on the social side that these successive declines generate the most fears. Indeed, the KICs have already paid the high price, by abolishing 5000 posts in five years. The new measures envisaged would lead them to abolish again several thousand posts. In addition to the obvious social difficulties resulting from such measures, the estimated cost of the 2000 full-time equivalents would, according to a report prepared in March 2018 by the General Inspectorate of Finance, the General Economic Council and General Economic and Financial Control, be EUR 200 million, in addition to the decrease in the TFC. Furthermore, the report of MPs Valérie Oppelt and Stella Dupont, which the National Assembly's joint information mission on chambers of commerce and industry adopted in July 2018, formally concludes that a further decline in the TFC is counterproductive. The Chair of the IAB Parliamentary Information Mission, Valérie Lacroute, called for the stability of the TFC in 2019. In total, the budget deficit thus stands at 107.5 billion, down by 8.5 billion euros from the level of deficit voted at first reading by the National Assembly.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u776	It aims to provide an additional 500,000 euros to IFRECOR, the French initiative for coral reefs. The IPCC report, the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change, released to the United Nations last October, reveals that, when the climate warms up by an additional 1.5 degrees, 70 to 90 per cent of the world's coral reefs will be endangered. France can pride itself on having been, thanks to IFRECOR, the origin of measures designed to protect, manage and preserve these particular spaces. I would point out that in the Committee on Finance, the draftsman of the opinion of the Committee on Sustainable Development had given an opinion in favour of this amendment adopted by his committee, as well as the various special rapporteurs of the Committee on Finance, but that the amendment had finally been rejected on the grounds that it was necessary to find funds within action 07 of programme 113 of the mission 'Sustainable ecology, development and mobility'. However, although I am looking for 500,000 euros in appropriations, I can't find them there. The allocation of a few thousand euros for the protection of such an important heritage area for humanity would be a signal – at a lower cost – that France could be proud to send at the end of the international year of coral reefs.

In this respect, Subamendment No 1296 is much more ambitious, I believe, than those proposed by the Members of the various benches. Indeed, it foresees a transition not from 2021 but from next year, moreover, it concerns not only palm oil but also other products which also contribute to deforestation. I therefore call on Members to vote for the abandonment of palm oil by providing social support for the hundreds of employees concerned. Subamendment No 1296 is a good balance.

They finance the water agencies and the transition of the bio plan. Here in this House, for the past 18 months, we have heard of pillar 1 and pillar 2, of the financing of bio, we hear that the transition must be carried out on one side and on the other, and therefore this is not financed in a stable way. For this reason, the Government has been proposing a stable resource for the past six months to finance the bio-plan. If we do not vote for the 50 million, we will not have the necessary funds for this plan and we will do as we have done for eighteen months or even for three or four years, and the means of financing the bio will still not be stabilized. We will therefore be hearing in the House from the many and repeated questions to the Government on how to finance bio. Therefore, I think we should follow the Government. ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u1626	The Senate has reintroduced the obligation for foreigners residing in France irregularly to pay a financial contribution in order to benefit from state medical aid. The reintroduction of this stamp duty will of course not solve the structural problems of the AME, but it is a first step and a symbolic measure to empower the foreign beneficiaries. It is indeed urgent to remove from the "supermarket" logic of non-compensatory rights. Many physicians point to the abuse of AME, such as the fact that its beneficiaries have access to dental treatments that are more aesthetic than therapeutic. It also took until 2011 to remove the thermal cures from the care basket. And how can we justify that foreigners illegally present in our territory receive such care without contributing while the French, who are heavily punctured to finance them, cannot always afford them? At present, one in three Frenchmen is abandoning care because of a lack of financial resources. The proposed measure is obviously insufficient when we see that the AME budget has been multiplied by twelve since 2000, from 75 million to 935 million euros for 300,000 beneficiaries, and that, despite significant increases, actual expenditure is constantly higher than budgeted. Thus, the overrun is 32 per cent for the period 2009-2015. [[Claims on several banks of the LaREM, SOC, FI and GDR groups]] I would add that only 10% of the files are currently controlled by the Caisse nationale d'assurance maladie, which leaves the door open to all abuses, and that an AME beneficiary costs on average about 3320 euros per year compared to 2900 euros for a Frenchman.

I salute the chairman of the committee and his teams, as well as the rapporteur-general with whom we have also worked a great deal and who, I believe, also deserves the applause of all the benches. I also welcome your presidency, Mr President, as well as that of the Vice-Presidents, with the help of the sitting department, and the staff under your authority, whether they are officials of the Assembly or, more particularly, those of the Committee on Finance. Finally, let me pay tribute to the teams of my own cabinet, Bruno Le Maire's and successive ministers and secretaries of state in the Government Bank, as well as to the staff of the various administrations. [[Applause on several benches of the LaREM group]] I am very pleased, in view of Thursday's final reading, that the Government is preparing to present this second five-year budget to France. Regardless of our differences, I think I can say that we have worked in a constructive and serene atmosphere, and I thank you for that.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-12-18-O1111.u1663	Next meeting, this afternoon, at 3 p.m.: Questions to the Government; Draft law authorizing the approval of the decision of the European Council for the election of Members of Parliament by direct universal suffrage. The meeting is adjourned.
